# Every stand-in value, with its source tier and its tweak level (GEN.725)

Status: generated from the stand-in registry of [gap-filling-plan.md](gap-filling-plan.md) by `scripts/build_stand_in_values.py`; do not edit it by hand (`python scripts/build_stand_in_values.py --write` rewrites it, and a test fails when it differs from the registry). The tier and the tweak level of each row are the registry's; a row's owner confirms them.

A stand-in is any value that is not a verified measurement of the thing it stands for. Every one carries two labels (Boss, 3:40 PM EDT on 2026-10-24; the tweak scale decided 4:01 PM EDT): **where its number came from** (the source tier) and **how far it was bent to give the outcome wanted** (the tweak level). Any change that adds a stand-in adds its row to the registry with both labels in the same pull request.

There are 978 rows below. A row may be a family of values (a recipe table, a set of bands); its tier cell then names the tier of each part, and its tweak is the highest level any part has.

## The source tiers

| Tier | Name | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| T1 | Verified | read in the source and the value is the source's |
| T2 | Confident unverified | from a source we trust but have not opened or cannot check |
| T3 | Marginal unverified | from a source we doubt, or a bracket from sources that disagree |
| T4 | Unverified | no source at all for the value, only a reason for its range |
| T5 | Confident educated guess based on physics | a law of physics fixes the form and the size to within a factor of a few |
| T6 | Marginal educated guess based on physics | physics fixes the form, but the size is argued, not derived |
| T7 | Educated guess based on inferred physics | the physics is itself inferred from other bodies or other regimes |
| T8 | Educated guess based on reputable fringe or new physics | a published but not yet accepted mechanism |
| T9 | Educated guess based on needed output (full tweak) | chosen so that the generated sky matches what has to come out |

Unverified and guessed tiers may be tweaked to hit target probabilities (Boss, 3:40 PM EDT on 2026-10-24).

## The tweak levels

| Level | Name | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | As envisioned | the value is used as its source gives it |
| L1 | Nudged inside the source's uncertainty | moved within the error bar the source prints |
| L2 | Tuned to a published statistic | moved until a published count or distribution is reproduced (a tuning target) |
| L3 | Hand-tuned against a sweep | chosen by running the generator over many draws and looking at the outcome |
| L4 | Fitted to the outcome with no independent source | the number is whatever makes the output right |

| All | 562 | 204 | 46 | 135 | 31 | 978 |

## How the labels were given

- **Tier** follows the kind of the fill and the evidence label of its inputs: a derived (D) fill from a source we read is T1 or T2; a bracket (B) is T2 or T3 by how many sources agree and whether we opened them; a theory (T) fill is T5 to T7 by how much of the physics is derived and how much inferred; a placeholder (P) with no source is T4 or, when physics argues its size, T6 or T7. Rows whose parts differ list each part.
- **Tweak** is L0 unless the value was moved after the fact. The L3 rows (hand-tuned against a sweep) are the mantle redox laws AT.a1 and a2, the atmosphere draw AT.b5, BC6 and AM19 (the exobase ratios); the L2 row is AM10 (the alkali activity, tuned to Kite et al. 2016); L1 rows are nudged inside the source's error bar. No row is L4: a row at L4 needs the item that justified it.
- The recipe rows of the atmosphere draw (`atmosphere_recipes_data.py`) are not rows here one by one. Those that carry a tuning target in `tuning_targets.json` (a3, a4, e1, f5, f7, k1 to k5, k7, o2) are L2: their weights were tuned to a published statistic.

## 6.3a Ranges of the galaxy shape dials

| Lever | Range | Label | Tier | Tweak | Source | Not read yet (item) | Burn-down (GEN.946) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--shape` | spiral | theory | T5 | L0 | the galaxy families; only the spiral exists, later families fill the list | - | - |
| `--mass` | 281 to 1.55e+11 solar masses | derived | T2 | L0 | Boss, 10-21 (7:04 to 7:31 PM EDT): floor half the stellar mass of Segue 1, 562 solar masses (Local Volume Database v1.1.1; Simon et al. 2011, arXiv:1007.4198); ceiling 1.5 x Andromeda's 1.03e11 (Sick et al. 2014, arXiv:1410.0017), read by the galaxy size research thread | - | - |
| `--compactness` | -3 to 3 sigma | derived | T2 | L0 | scatter of the late-type size-mass law, Shen et al. 2003 (read in the repository); three sigma is the draw's own cap | - | - |
| `--density` | 0.04 to 100000 solar masses per pc^2 | derived | T2 | L0 | density-limits report (10-21): the smallest density with a planned sector at a 4 pc scale height is 0.043; the largest found in dense stellar systems is 1e5 (Hopkins et al. 2010, arXiv:0908.4088); the floor also grows with the scale height (check_outline) | - | - |
| `--disk-scale-length` | 28 to 8250 pc | derived | T2 | L0 | Boss, 10-21 (7:04 PM to 8:08 PM EDT): floor 28 pc (Segue 1's half, 7.1 pc, is under the 4 pc cell grid's reach: a variable sector edge, a later item, would bring it back); ceiling 1.5 x Andromeda's 5.5 kpc (Courteau et al. 2011, arXiv:1106.3564; Dorman et al. 2013, arXiv:1310.4179), read by the galaxy size research thread | - | - |
| `--flatness` | 3 to 20 | bracket | T3 | L0 | median 7.3 and 0.13 dex read in Kregel, van der Kruit and de Grijs 2002 (shape_draw); the range 3 to 20 is the proposal's suggestion (galaxy size levers, 10-21), not read in a source | - | - |
| `--bulge-share` | 0 to 0.9 | bracket | T3 | L0 | stage medians from Graham and Worley 2008 Table 4 (shape_draw); the range 0 to 0.9 is the proposal's suggestion (galaxy size levers, 10-21), not read in a source | - | - |
| `--bulge-size` | 0.02 to 1 | bracket | T3 | L0 | bar length from Erwin 2005 Table 5 and Gadotti 2011 (shape_draw), which draws 0.067 to 1; the floor of 0.02 is the proposal's suggestion (galaxy size levers, 10-21) | - | - |
| `--nuclear-amplitude` | 0 to 10 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | hand option of the first plan, no source | - | - |
| `--edge` | 4 to 262140 pc, or 3 to 20 L | derived | T2 | L0 | relative: Boss, 10-21 (7:01 PM EDT), 3 to 20 scale lengths; absolute: one 4 pc shell up to the grid's reach of 65,535 shells, 262 kpc (spherical.MAX_ADDRESSABLE_SHELL) | - | - |
| `--halo-ratio` | 10 to 3000 | derived | T2 | L0 | the relation's own range: a halo holds 26 times its stars at the peak of star formation efficiency (Moster et al. 2013, read in the repository) and 2200 times at the halo threshold of 1e10 solar masses; the ends are those, rounded | - | - |
| `--stellar-halo-share` | 0.001 to 0.3 | bracket | T3 | L0 | the draw's own cut: 0.1 percent (Dragonfly galaxies with no detected halo) to 30 percent (D'Souza and Bell 2014's disc-dominated galaxies), read in structure_draw | - | - |
| `--richness` | 1 to 3 systems per solar mass | bracket | T3 | L0 | the derived mean system mass runs 0.47 (old stars) to 0.79 solar masses (stars just formed), 1.3 to 2.1 systems per solar mass (the generator's own laws); the band 1 to 3 widens it by about a factor of 1.4 | - | - |
| `--arm-count` | 1 to 10 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | Hart et al. 2017 counts 1 to 5 or more arms; the ceiling of 10 is a placeholder | - | - |
| `--pitch-angle-deg` | -90 to 90 degrees, at least 1 away from 0 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | Hart 2017 draws 10 to 40; the full quarter turn is a placeholder; under 1 degree from 0 is refused because a flat pitch divides by zero (density-limits report, 10-21) | - | - |
| `--arm-contrast` | 0 to 1 | placeholder | T4 | L0 | 0 to 1 because the density cannot go negative (theory); the 0.4 default is the first plan's hand value, no source | - | - |

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | replaced_by | evidence | Not read yet (item) | Burn-down (GEN.946) |

| SP2 | T | T3 | L1 | spin.accretion_spin | isotropic or prograde, fixed | a rocky planet | a measured obliquity distribution | the obliquity after a giant impact is isotropic, n(e) = sin(e)/2, which comes from components of one width (Kokubo and Genda 2010); a planet built by planetesimals alone spins prograde, the sense of the shear that feeds it (Schlichting and Sari 2007, recalled), so its z component is folded positive; the fold is the full effect where the paper finds a bias | - | - |
| SP3 | T | T7 | L0 | spin.accretion_spin | scale by breakup, fixed | a rocky planet of any density | the paper's mass and density scan | the paper finds the mean spin independent of mass at one density; a planet of another density is scaled by its own breakup rate sqrt(G M / R^3), which is assumed, not read | - | - |
| SP4 | P | T6 | L0 | spin.BREAKUP_FRACTION | 0.7 to 1 | a rocky planet | a source on the spin of a planet at the end of accretion | a birth spin is held at 0.85 of the breakup rate, the cap the hot stars use (planetGen's choice); about 14 percent of the Gaussian vectors lie above it | - | - |
| SP5 | P | T3 | L1 | constants.ROTATION_PERIOD_RANGE_HOURS | 8 to 48 and 8 to 20 | moons; gas giants | a circumplanetary-disk spin law for moons; a giant spin-up law | a moon's birth spin is log-uniform over 8 to 48 hours (the range this generator has used for rocky bodies since GEN.104, whose source was the impact papers), a gas giant's uniform over 8 to 20 hours (Jupiter 9.9, Saturn 10.7, Neptune 16.1, Uranus 17.2 h, recalled); the tide then moves both | - | - |
| SP6 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_spin.ROCKY_MOMENT_FACTOR, GIANT_MOMENT_FACTOR | 0.25 to 0.4 | rocky bodies; gas giants | a factor from the body's core fraction | the moment-of-inertia factor I / (m R^2): Earth 0.331, Venus 0.33, Mars 0.365, Mercury 0.346, the Moon 0.393 (rock, 0.33); Jupiter 0.254, Saturn 0.22 (giants, 0.25); recalled | - | - |
| SP7 | T | T3 | L0 | body_spin.dissipation_of | none | a body spun down to the orbital rate | the layered-planet response with an ocean or a molten core | the despinning torque is the rheology's: `k2/Q` at the semi-diurnal frequency `2 (spin - n)` in magnitude of the body's own mantle (`spin_torques.rheology_dissipation` at `interior_heat.tide_temperature_k`, GEN.957), so a hot body despins fast and a cold stiff one slowly, and a gas giant keeps a fixed pair (`tidal_heating.GIANT_K2`, `GIANT_TIDAL_Q`) while ice has its shell's (`icy_tide.k2_over_q`, GEN.960); Gladman et al. 1996 (Icarus 122:166, recalled) is the constant-Q special case | - | - |
| SP8 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_spin.RESONANCES | the window edges | a rocky planet brought down from a fast spin | the same windows for a layered planet | READ through a summary of Makarov 2012 (ApJ 752:73, arXiv:1110.2658): with a frequency-dependent rheology capture is certain in 0.34 < e < 0.49 for 2:1, 0.2 < e < 0.41 for 3:2 and e < 0.29 for 1:1; the first window met coming down takes the spin | - | - |
| SP9 | P | T3 | L1 | tidal_spin.RESONANCE_CAPTURE_PROBABILITY | 0.07 to 1 | Mercury-like captures; captured moons with e of 0.2 to 0.5 | a rheology-dependent capture probability from one model | capture inside a window is a chance (GEN.1172): 0.3 for the 3:2 and 0.1 for the 2:1, decided by two uniforms the body keeps from its birth spin; the sources split (Makarov 2012: 40 of 40; Goldreich and Peale 1966: 0.07 at Mercury's eccentricity with a constant-torque tide; Correia and Laskar 2004: 0.55 with the eccentricity's chaotic history), 3:2 is the middle of 0.07 and 0.55 rounded down and 2:1 a third of it, an educated guess from the sources; the synchronous state is caught for sure | - | - |
| SP10 | T | T6 | L0 | planets.generate_spin | none | every planet and moon | a coupled spin, obliquity and eccentricity history | approximations of the spin state: a locked body turns exactly once an orbit (not 1 + 6 e^2 of a constant-time-lag tide); the eccentricity is the stored one, not the larger past one; the obliquity stays as drawn until the spin has come all the way down, then is the Cassini-state tilt of SP22; the planet's air is a thermal tide (SP13) and the moons are in the sum from GEN.959, but there is no core-mantle coupling | - | - |
| SP13 | T | T6 | L0 | body_spin.thermal_tide_of | none | rocky planets with air | a thermal tide for a moon with air and for a giant's envelope | the thermal tide of the air acts on a rocky planet with a stored surface pressure above 0 and starlight above 0; not on a moon (its planet holds it), an icy body or a gas giant (its air has no surface) | - | - |
| SP14 | T | T6 | L0 | planets.generate_spin | none | planets held away from synchronism by the air | the obliquity history under the thermal tide | an asynchronous balance of the tides is reported at obliquity zero, prograde (tilt 0) if the rate is positive and retrograde (tilt 180) if it is negative (Venus), whatever tilt it was born with | - | - |
| SP17 | P | T1 | L0 | spin.KRAFT_BREAK_LOW_K, KRAFT_BREAK_HIGH_K | 6400 to 6700 K | a main-sequence dwarf of solar metallicity | a break by mass and metallicity (GEN.1163) | READ in full, Beyer and White 2024 (arXiv:2408.02638): the Kraft break is centred at 6550 K with a width of about 200 K, from 6450 to 6650 K (uncertainty about 50 K on the limits), 1.32 to 1.41 solar masses; the 217 stars cooler than it have a mean v sin i of 6.7 km/s (standard deviation 3.6), the 40 hotter ones 63 km/s (standard deviation 35) | - | - |
| SP18 | P | T1 | L0 | spin.HOT_STAR_BREAK_SPEED_KMS | 60 to 100 km/s | a dwarf of 1.41 solar masses or less above the break | a speed by mass from stars between the break and 1.6 solar masses | the mean v sin i of the stars just hotter than the break, 63 km/s (SP17), deprojected by 4 / pi for random orientation (the mean of sin i): about 80 km/s, kept as the median equatorial speed at the top of the break | - | - |
| SP19 | P | T1 | L0 | spin.HOT_STAR_SPEED_KMS, HOT_STAR_SPEED_SIGMA, HOT_STAR_FULL_SPEED_MASS_SOL | 160 to 200 km/s; width 0.3 to 0.45 | a dwarf of 1.8 solar masses or more | a speed rising with mass above 1.8 solar masses (the paper's Table 4 trend, about 46 km/s per solar mass in the mode) | READ, Zorec and Royer 2012 (A&A 537:A120, arXiv:1201.2052, Table 4, read from the paper's text): the 1.60 to 2.00 solar mass bin (191 stars) has a Maxwellian mode of the fast rotators of 160 km/s and a dispersion of 54 km/s, a mean of 2 / sqrt(pi) times the mode, 180 km/s; the mode rises to 238 km/s at 3.15 to 3.85 solar masses, which the model does not follow (hot stars of every mass above 1.8 keep 180 km/s with a log-normal width of 0.45, a planetGen choice wider than the paper's 54 / 160); their lowest bin starts at 1.6 solar masses | - | - |
| SP20 | P | T6 | L0 | spin.KRAFT_BREAK_TOP_MASS_SOL, HOT_STAR_FULL_SPEED_MASS_SOL, spin.hot_star_median_speed_kms | a straight line in v or in ln v | a dwarf of 1.41 to 1.8 solar masses | the same speed by mass from a sample of F and early A stars with masses | between the top of the break (1.41 solar masses, SP18's 80 km/s) and the first mass the paper covers (SP19) the median speed is interpolated, linear in ln v over mass; no source covers the stars of 1.41 to 1.6 solar masses (the break paper gives one mean for all hotter stars); the interpolation is a bridge between two read anchors, not fitted | - | - |
| SP21 | P | T6 | L0 | spin.HOT_STAR_PEAK_MASS_SOL, HOT_STAR_PEAK_SPEED_KMS, HOT_STAR_FALL_MASS_SOL | peak 240 to 290 km/s; fall mass 6 to 10 solar masses | a dwarf of 1.8 solar masses or more | a speed by mass from a table of mean equatorial speeds of B stars with their masses | GEN.1170: above 1.8 solar masses the median runs up to 268 km/s at 3.5 solar masses (Zorec and Royer 2012, Table 4: the last bin, 3.15 to 3.85 solar masses, has a mode of 238 km/s, a mean 1.128 times it; the mode rises about 46 km/s per solar mass from 1.8), then falls back to 180 km/s at 8 solar masses and stays there (READ, Huang, Gies and McSwain 2010, ApJ 722:605, arXiv:1008.1761, Section 5: the share of newborn B stars under half the critical speed is 37%, 53% and 84% for 2 to 4, 4 to 8 and 8 solar masses and more, the whole sample's mode 0.49 of the critical speed; the paper gives shares, not speeds, so the fall to 180 km/s is a stand-in: 84% under half of a critical speed of about 580 km/s puts the median near 175 km/s, with the critical speed read from a summary of the paper's tables, not verified); the line between the anchors is straight in ln v | - | - |
| SP22 | P | T3 | L3 | tidal_spin.CASSINI_COUPLING_RANGE | 0.001 to 0.01 low end; 1 high end | a locked or resonant planet or moon; falsifier: the obliquity of a measured locked body (Mercury 0.034, the Moon's state 2, Io and Europa under 0.1 degrees) outside the draw's range, or a Laplace-Lagrange run of the generated system | the nodal regression g from the system's secular theory and alpha from the body's measured or modelled J2 | GEN.1165: the obliquity of a locked or resonant body is its first Cassini state (Peale 1969, recalled): the axis precesses about the orbit normal at alpha, the normal about the Laplace plane's at g, and the axis settles at `eps = x i` with `x = abs(g) / (alpha + abs(g))` between 0 (a shape that holds the axis) and 1 (no hold); `x` is drawn log-uniform over 0.003 to 1, the low end Mercury's 0.034 degrees (2.04 arcmin, Margot et al. 2012, recalled) against about 8 degrees to its Laplace plane; the generator has no secular frequencies or fossil shapes to give alpha and g, and a hydrostatic alpha is far under a real planet's; the spread was chosen so a locked body's median tilt is a fraction of a degree and its p95 under 5 (sweep row 7 of GEN.753) | - | no data |

| RB1 | T | T3 | L0 | radiation_belts.WIND_EDGE_FRACTION | 0.6 to 0.9 | a body with a dipole in a stellar wind | a drift-shell model of where the magnetopause shadows the belts | the outer edge of a wind-limited body is 0.75 of the magnetopause standoff, which puts Earth's at 7.3 (the outer belt ends at about 40 000 km; recalled); the standoff is the Chapman-Ferraro scaling of `magnetism.magnetopause_rp` | GEN.983 | - |
| RB2 | T | T4 | L0 | radiation_belts.ROTATION_EDGE_FACTOR | 7 to 12 | a fast rotator with a strong field | a centrifugal-balance model of the plasma sheet | the outer edge of a spin-limited body is 9 times the synchronous distance, beyond which the plasma no longer turns with the body: Jupiter's synchronous distance is 2.24 radii and its belts reach about 20 (recalled; Saturn would be 16) | GEN.983 | - |
| RB3 | P | T3 | L1 | radiation_belts.WIND_PEAK_FRACTION, ROTATION_PEAK_FRACTION | 0.5 to 0.7 and 0.2 to 0.35 | the two limits | AE-9 and Divine-Garrett peak positions as functions of the field | the electron belt peaks at 0.62 of the outer edge for a wind-limited body (Earth's peak at 4 to 5 against 7.3) and at 0.25 of it for a spin-limited one (Jupiter's peak inside Io's orbit, 5.9, against 20); recalled, two bodies fit | GEN.983 | - |
| RB4 | P | T3 | L1 | radiation_belts.INNER_PEAK_FRACTION | 0.3 to 0.45 | a body with air or a surface to make the albedo neutrons | a cosmic-ray albedo neutron decay (CRAND) source model | the inner proton belt peaks at 0.35 of the electron peak (Earth's at 1.5 to 2 against 4.5); recalled, one body fits | GEN.983 | - |
| RB5 | D | T4 | L0 | radiation_belts.ATMOSPHERE_EDGE_SCALE_HEIGHTS | 35 to 60 | a body with air | a bounce-loss lifetime against the density profile | the inner edge of a body with air is 45 scale heights above the surface, where the air stops a bouncing particle (Earth's belts begin near 400 km, 1.06 radii); the scale height is `R T / (M g)` at the surface temperature | GEN.983 | - |
| RB6 | P | T6 | L3 | radiation_belts.EARTH_OUTER_PEAK_GY_YR, EARTH_INNER_PEAK_GY_YR, INTENSITY_EXPONENT | 2000 to 6000 Gy; 0.5 to 1.2 | a dipole body in a quiet wind | AP-9 and AE-9, Divine-Garrett and the Juno and Galileo dose measurements | the intensity of each belt at its peak is Earth's 3000 Gy per year behind 100 mil of aluminium (about 300 krad; recalled from the orders of AP-8 and AE-8 in the shielded orbit literature) times the dipole moment to the 0.8, which puts Jupiter's belt some 2700 times Earth's, inside the 1e2 to 1e5 the Galileo and Juno dose records span; the exponent was fitted to that range, not derived | GEN.983 | no data |
| RB7 | T | T6 | L3 | radiation_belts.OUTER_RISE_EXPONENT, INNER_RISE_EXPONENT, FALL_EXPONENT, EDGE_EXPONENT | 2 to 6 | the main belts of a dipole | tabulated flux maps in `B` and `L` | the profile of a belt rises as the fourth power (electrons) or the second (protons) of the distance past the inner edge, falls as the sixth power of `L` past the peak and is cut to nothing at the outer edge; shapes chosen so Earth has a slot (a tenth of the peak at L 2.5) and Io to Europa is about a factor 10 (Galileo: 6.7; recalled) | GEN.983 | no data |
| RB8 | T | T6 | L0 | radiation_belts.belts_for | none | every body with a field | a coupled field, plasma and belt model | approximations: a dipole aligned with the spin; dose behind one aluminium thickness only; no solar-cycle, storm or longitude dependence; moons, rings and plasma tori are not in it | - | - |
